## 0. Where we are going now (as of Sep 26, 2026 19:00 EDT; everything below this block is history). This is the base document for writing the paper.

**Citation audit (Sep 27, this worktree):** `.claude/analyses/citation-check-2026-09-27.md` checks all 44 bibliography entries against primary records; 29 cited: 18 VERIFIED, 9 FIX (including optional publication updates), 2 UNPUBLISHED, none NOT FOUND or UNRESOLVED. Bibliography and TeX unchanged; author lists differ between arXiv and published records for PixArt and SD3.

**Paper.** CoRL 2026 PhysWM workshop, 4 pages. Deadline Sep 30 23:59 AoE = **Thursday Oct 1, 07:59 EDT**; internal cutoff Wednesday Sep 30 midnight; draft to Changliu Sunday Sep 27 evening (SD 3.5 numbers provisional); final tables Monday and Tuesday. Authors Rohan Nagabhirava and Keerthana Chirumamilla. Draft lives in `paper/main.tex` (about 3,900 words today, on the Sep 24 spine; to be rewritten to the spine below).

**The spine (finalised Sep 26 19:15 with two independent framing reviews, `.claude/analyses/framing-review-opus-2026-09-26.md`, `framing-review-astra-2026-09-26.md`).** An evaluation paper. Contribution sentence: an open 30-map Doom evaluation shows that world models trained on four maps under one recipe beat copy-last-frame persistence on held-out episodes of those maps but not on maps they never saw; the deficit sits in the predicted latents rather than the decoder, grows with a distance from the map's footage to the training footage (qualified), and leaves the turn response intact; one-step quality does not predict closed-loop stability, which the EMA reduces but does not remove. Three backbones as a comparison of pretrained packages, not the headline.

1. **Headline, reframed Sep 26 19:10 EDT after the Opus distance review (verified by main from the frozen h1 metrics): the model's advantage over copy-last shrinks on unseen arenas; the raw-pixel sign flip is the decoder's floor showing through.** In the decoded comparison (model and copy-last both decoded, scored against the decoded true frame, so the decoder's floor cancels) the U-Net 200k EMA beats copy-last on every arena: +3.2 to +4.3 dB on the four training maps, +0.7 to +2.7 dB on the 13 unseen arenas, so the advantage drops by about 2 dB off the training maps (campaign maps: -0.6 to +1.6). Against raw-frame persistence the model's output must pass through the decoder (reconstruction ceiling 22.7 to 24.7 dB), so on static-footage arenas (persistence 22 to 23 dB) it falls below persistence (raw gain -0.9 to +1.3 across the unseen arenas, tracking persistence PSNR at -0.78, not the map shift), while on the training maps it stays above (+0.5 to +1.8). LPIPS is different (Opus cohesion review, verified): the stock decoder's own LPIPS (0.08 to 0.12) is below persistence's (0.15 to 0.23), so there is room to beat copy-last perceptually, and the model does on 4 of 4 training maps (-0.006 to -0.056) but loses on 13 of 13 unseen arenas (+0.06 to +0.16), still 13 of 13 when scored against the decoded true frame; the perceptual flip is real and is not the decoder floor: under uncertainty the model predicts a blurred average, which PSNR rewards and LPIPS penalises. At four tics the decoded PSNR advantage also goes negative on arenas 6 and 7. The HUD (32 rows the stock decoder renders at about 18 dB while persistence copies it exactly) carries 39 to 68 percent of the ceiling's error, so scene-only (rows 0 to 207) columns are needed beside full-frame ones. Cohesive statement (three views converged, `.claude/analyses/evaluation-cohesion-decision-2026-09-26.md`): the model's skill over copy-last shrinks off the training maps in every space; in squared error it stays positive at one tic; perceptually it flips on every unseen arena; raw-PSNR sign changes track motion, not the shift. Presentation: the in-domain cross-backbone table in pixels through the tuned decoder against raw persistence (latent spaces differ across backbones); latent skill primary within a backbone (unseen arenas, adaptation curves); both decoders scored on the same predictions once; per-window rows with MSEs, four decoders, full-frame and scene-only, `copy_lpips_dec`, duplicate flags, fp16 predicted latents, h1 and h4; CIs by bootstrap over episodes. All numbers to be recomputed on the fresh single-protocol set. Figure 1 is the per-map figure sorted by distance with the decoder's reconstruction ceiling marked. Lead with LPIPS and the decoder-free latent ratio, because on three near-static campaign maps (19, 25, 26) the decoder's reconstruction of the true frame is itself below raw persistence in PSNR. Reference is raw-frame persistence (`persist_psnr_raw`), never the decoded copy of the last frame (`copy_psnr_raw`), which scores about 1.4 dB lower because it passes through the decoder and so inflates every gain.
2. **Three legs, shown side by side, never summed, no "shared physics" claim:** (a) control response: the directional check (swap TURN_LEFT/TURN_RIGHT in the newest control) reverses the predicted camera motion at the right magnitude in 0.85 of windows for the U-Net 200k EMA and 0.86 for PixArt 155k, and survives on unseen maps (per-map 30-map run in progress: 0.79 to 0.87 so far); (b) latent prediction error: the copy-last latent MSE ratio per window (30-map rescore in progress); (c) rendering: the stock decoder's reconstruction ceiling is flat, 23.91 dB on training maps against 23.71 on unseen maps, so the unseen-map loss is in the predicted latents; ceiling-minus-model grows from 1.55 to 2.41 dB. The decoder-adaptation arm is cancelled; the training-map decoder tunes (tonight) give the tuned-decoder column and the ceiling per space; a paired stock-versus-tuned decoding of the same predictions (Sunday) answers "is it decoder loss".
3. **Distance, qualified.** D(map) = motion-weighted sliced Wasserstein distance between the map's per-frame SD 1.x latent cloud and the nearest training map's cloud, frozen Sep 25. U-Net 200k, 30 maps, one tic: partial Spearman of gain over persistence on D controlling for persistence PSNR -0.73 [-0.84, -0.32], permutation p 1e-4, four tics -0.80; pixel-space and SD 3.5-space distances agree at rank 0.86 to 0.90. The pre-declared gate says "does not support" because check (ii) fails on 8 campaign maps (disjoint 4-episode subsets disagree by 11 to 29 percent) and check (iv) fails (arena 7 at 0.28 is farther than 11 campaign maps); the paper states the failed gate and the amended assessment "qualified evidence". Structural controls: training-map indicator -0.56; plus map family -0.42 with a CI crossing zero; **within the 13 unseen arenas the zero-shot gain does not track D at all (Spearman about -0.02, partial on persistence about -0.1; two independent recomputations)**, so the relation lives in the training-versus-unseen and arena-versus-campaign contrasts. Amended CIs (Freedman-Lane, four-tic covariate) to be rerun on Spiderman with the per-window files.
4. **Closed loop.** SD 3.5 live weights have an intermittent absorbing failure (a latent channel's mean captured at a fixed value, blank frames); collapse rates 50k to 130k over matched windows: frame under 10 dB in 23 of 256 live rollouts against 9 of 256 EMA (11 against 8 outside the 70k collapse), so the EMA removes checkpoint-specific collapses, not the failure mode. EMA versus live in closed loop is new in the literature; expect the DIAMOND objection (few-step sampling drives drift) and answer it.
5. **The three rows (one recipe: next tic at 35 Hz, 32-tic channel-stacked context, executed 19-button control history, v-prediction, noise augmentation, 200k updates of batch 32, lr 5e-5, fp32 EMA 0.9999).** One tic / four tics on 512 held-out training-map windows (persistence 21.57 / 0.203 and 19.21 / 0.354): U-Net 200k EMA 22.49 / 0.177 and 21.33 / 0.224; SD 3.5 140k EMA 23.26 / 0.133 and 21.59 / 0.197 (200k about Mon 00:30 EDT); PixArt 155k EMA 22.41 / 0.184 and 21.24 / 0.235 (200k tonight about 20:15 EDT). 256-tic rollouts against copy-seed (17.52): SD 3.5 140k EMA 18.03; PixArt 150k EMA 17.41, live 15.97. The rows land within about 0.8 dB of each other; the findings come from how we evaluate.

**Related work (all verified from primary sources Sep 26; one-page map `.claude/analyses/related-work-map-2026-09-26.md`, five slice memos beside it).** Position against **XEWorld** (arXiv 2608.05799, Aug 2026: held-out robot embodiments; appearance distance predicts held-out error r = 0.81, kinematic distance does not; full fine-tuning on 25 to 75 episodes causes catastrophic forgetting; no persistence baseline, five targets). Unseen-scene evaluation of world models is rare, on 1 to 5 domains, pooled, never per scene, never against persistence (AVID, AdaWorld, Pathdreamer, Vista, GAIA-2); no Doom paper does it (GameNGen holds out trajectories; MultiGen trains on 100 generated maps and never says its test maps are held out). Copy-last persistence was the 2015 to 2019 video-prediction convention (PredNet on an unseen dataset, Villegas 2019) and vanished from game world models; the ceiling / copy-last / model table exists in occupancy and feature forecasting (OccWorld, DINO-Foresight). The map-holdout practice comes from the Doom agent literature (Arnold trains on 2 to 5 and tests on 6 to 8; the ViZDoom competition's unseen maps).

**The next study (October claim; a four-map U-Net pilot Sunday as a preliminary figure): what does it take to cross the map shift.** Adapt the four-map model to each unseen map with LoRA on frozen backbone weights from a few episodes, plot gain over persistence against LoRA updates and against episodes, and test whether D predicts the cost. Design page `docs/lora_adaptation_design_2026-09-26.html` (artifact 7wEKjvwYaKFbSEEruGBk6J) with eight decisions; Astra's design review `.claude/analyses/astra-adaptation-review-2026-09-26.md`; three independent dataset views (main, Astra, Opus) in `*-dataset-view-2026-09-26.md`. Decided or converged: targets are the **13 unseen deathmatch arenas** (1, 6 to 17; Arnold's WAD holds MAP01 to 17 only; campaign maps stay zero-shot because their footage does not cover the level); **a fresh 20-episode set per arena** under one pinned protocol (same Arnold build, 8 bots, 150 s, every tic, pre-declared seeds, each worker's first episode discarded because Arnold's weapon switch executes only there), the old episodes remaining the source of D and of the zero-shot points; **split 12 adapt (nested 1, 2, 4, 6, 12; 6 for the step curve) and 8 held out**, identical on every map, 32 fixed windows per held-out episode, step 0 rescored on them; rank 16 alpha 16 LoRA on attention projections (MLP as a flag) with the control MLP, positions, input projection and noise embedding trained in full (DiffFit: on DiT-XL/2 LoRA alone reaches FID 81 against 16.6 full), lr 1e-4, fp32 EMA 0.999, global batch 32 kept (fit check decides one card, accumulation or two cards), step grid 0/250/500/1,000/2,000; source weights = the snapshot's EMA tensors (the trainer's init path loads live weights; fixed in the adapter path); cost reported at 25/50/75 percent of the training-map gain plus gain at 2,000 steps and area under the curve, with Astra's alternative (latent skill ratio R = model over copy-last latent error, cost = first budget with R <= 0.90) as the decoder-free primary if adopted; maps that never cross are right-censored; guards: directional score on the map and training-map validation gain (forgetting) at every point, forgetting also plotted against D; pre-registered additions: a directed nearest-training-latent distance as a second x axis (symmetric OT predicts transfer worse than coverage: Mensink, Westny) and an incremental test that D predicts cost beyond zero-shot error and motion. Known limits to state: the arena range is 0.115 to 0.282 with arena 7 carrying two thirds of it (about 40 percent power at n = 13); the October fix is distance-stratified Obsidian-generated arenas chosen before any result. Pilot: maps 8, 16, 6, 7, U-Net, two seeds, on existing episodes, kept out of the headline. Full fine-tune reference on one far map when a card is free. Open with Rohan: the recording go (273 episodes, about 65 GB raw, 1 h CPU tonight, 4.5 A6000-hours of SD 1 encoding Sunday), whether a 4-map campaign stress arm (never pooled) stays for October, the Superman copy route (key or password relay; otherwise the pilot runs on Spiderman GPU 1 after 14:00 Sunday), and decisions 5 and 6 on the page.

**Single-dataset rule (Rohan, Sep 26 evening) and the distance problem.** The paper reports one dataset: the 17 arenas of Arnold's deathmatch WAD (training maps 2 to 5 on their 25 validation episodes; the 13 unseen arenas on a fresh 20-episode set recorded under one protocol), one set of distances and one set of results, all recomputed on that set; the earlier recording batches and the campaign maps stay in the dataset release and out of the paper. Within the 13 unseen arenas the frozen frame distance orders nothing (gain on D about 0.0) while gain tracks persistence PSNR at -0.78 and the model's absolute PSNR tracks persistence at 0.94: D is an appearance distance that separates map families, not maps within a family; the model predicts transitions. Astra is on an unanchored design round (`.claude/analyses/astra-brief-distance-rethink-2026-09-26.md`) for a transition-level or coverage distance to be validated on zero-shot data and frozen before the adaptation runs. Cost target (decision 7), revised 19:45 EDT at Rohan's direction (pixel level, decoder involved, anchored in domain; three views synthesised in `.claude/analyses/cost-target-decision-2026-09-26.md`): headline A = the decoded advantage over copy-last (Genie delta-PSNR form) through the tuned SD 1 decoder on the scene rows, line at the training maps' own advantage (3.6 dB stock today) with crossings at 25/50/100 percent and 50 percent the headline cost; second panel B = the perceptual margin against raw persistence (LPIPS) at the training maps' margin with zero drawn; table column C = the gap to the decoder's best render at the training maps' gap; latent skill to the appendix for LoRA versus full fine-tune only; lines frozen from step-0 predictions on the training maps (stock tonight, tuned once the SD 1 decoder is gated), before any adapted score. Half the raw PSNR gain was rejected because four arenas already exceed it at step 0. Scope (decision 6): four-map pilot only; the 13-arena curve is October. Full fine-tune comparator (decision 5): October unless the pilot's arena 7 is flat and a card idles Monday. Live weights are the primary for the step curve (EMA 0.999 lags). The one page: `docs/lora_adaptation_design_2026-09-26.html` (artifact 7wEKjvwYaKFbSEEruGBk6J).

*Sep 25 spine (history):*

**Paper (CoRL 2026 PhysWM workshop, Sep 30 AoE, 4 pages; draft to Changliu Sunday Sep 27 evening, final tables Sep 28 to 29).** Authors Rohan Nagabhirava and Keerthana Chirumamilla. The spine, decided Sep 24 to 25:

1. **A matched-recipe comparison of three backbones as Doom world models** on one public dataset: SD 1.4 U-Net (4-channel latents), SD 3.5 Medium (DiT, 16-channel latents), PixArt-alpha (DiT, 4-channel latents); every tic (35 Hz), 32-tic context, executed-control history, v-prediction, 200k updates of batch 32 each. The DiT-versus-U-Net question stays as the main table; it is no longer the whole contribution, because the rows land within about 0.6 dB of each other and the findings came from how we evaluate.
2. **Evaluation against a copy-last-frame persistence reference on the same windows.** One tic ahead on held-out episodes of the training maps (persistence 21.57 dB / 0.203 LPIPS): U-Net 200k EMA 22.49 / 0.177 (+0.92 dB; +2.1 dB at four tics); SD 3.5 100k EMA 23.06 / 0.142 (+1.49; +2.18 at four tics; it passed the U-Net's final LPIPS at 40k and both its final numbers at 50k); PixArt 95k EMA +0.71 (+1.86 at four tics). SD 3.5's EMA 256-tic rollouts sit above the copy-seed baseline at every horizon. No 2024 to 2026 world-model paper reports such a reference (5.9 of the dossier); on unseen maps the U-Net is below it at one tic.
3. **The map-distance generalization study** (`.claude/analyses/distance-study-design-2026-09-24.md`, results frozen under `results/distance_study/`): each map is a motion-weighted cloud of per-frame SD 1.x latents, its distance is the sliced Wasserstein distance to the nearest training map, the outcome is the model's PSNR gain over persistence on that map. U-Net 200k over 30 maps: partial Spearman (controlling for motion) -0.73 [-0.84, -0.32] at one tic and -0.80 [-0.88, -0.46] at four tics, permutation p 1e-4, leave-one-map-out same sign on all 30, replicated in pixel space (map ranking agrees at 0.86), no relation between episodes of one map. Pre-registered and frozen before scoring. **Open:** the pre-declared verdict gate prints "does not support" because two distance sanity checks failed (same-WAD arenas not all nearer than campaign maps; 4-episode subsets of eight campaign maps disagree by more than 10 percent); Rohan rules, Astra reviews Sep 26 13:30 EDT. SD 3.5 and PixArt are scored through the study at their final 200k.
4. **Two closed-loop findings.** Under autoregression the SD 3.5 live weights have an intermittent absorbing failure (a latent channel's mean captured at a fixed value while the scale stays normal, blank frames; 8 to 15 of 16 rollouts at 50k and 70k) that the fp32 EMA weights suffer far less but not never: on the same 16 windows from 55k to 130k, a frame under 10 dB occurs in 23 of 256 live rollouts against 9 of 256 EMA rollouts, and outside the 70k collapse 11 against 8, so the EMA removes the checkpoint-specific collapses rather than the failure mode (Astra review Sep 26); and a directional check on real turning windows (swap TURN_LEFT/TURN_RIGHT in the newest control) reverses the predicted camera motion in 87 percent of windows for the U-Net and 84 percent for SD 3.5 at the right magnitude, so neither learned persistence.

Steward 7 reads every 5k on GPU 3 (TF, rollouts, probes, directional) and reports to the main session; every run streams to W&B `doomdit-nexttic`.

**Dataset (public on Hugging Face):** 6,000 training episodes on arenas 2 to 5 encoded in both spaces (rows train on ids 0:2000); validation 6000:6100, test 7000:7100 (sealed); evaluation corpus of 210 seeded episodes on 30 maps (seen: 60 on the original 17 maps; unseen: 20 on arenas 16 and 17; unseen2: 130 on 13 campaign maps) per tic in the SD 1.x space, SD 3.5 space encoding.

*Sep 16 framing (history):*



**Framing (decided with Rohan, Sep 16).** The paper is a warm-start study for small-budget world models, with Doom as the testbed, delivered on the first open reproducible Doom benchmark. Headline: pretraining exposure in the target latent space, not architecture, predicts small-budget Doom world-model quality. Supporting findings: (a) map transfer, where the U-Net's advantage concentrates on the maps it trained on and the transformer gives back less on unseen maps (two maps today, 17 once the new corpus is scored); (b) evaluation, where every model falls to or below the frozen-seed persistence reference perceptually by horizon 64, so long-horizon metrics need persistence references. Explicitly not claimed: first DiT for Doom (PlayGen 2024), LPIPS parity with GameNGen, "stable dynamics", "superior generalization", pretraining as the proven cause. General lessons for small-budget single-environment world models go in the conclusion: pick the warm start by exposure to your latent space; architecture is second-order at this budget; report rollouts against persistence; measure transfer to unseen scenes.

**Rows under one recipe** (90k updates, batch 32, lr 5e-5, warmup 2000, context 32, v-prediction, SD KL-f8 latents): DiT-XL/2 ImageNet (two seeds, done), SD 1.4 U-Net (done), PixArt-alpha 512 (done, evaluating Sep 16), UniDiffuser v1 (LAION-2B transformer in our latent space; build started Sep 16, train Sep 17 on GPU 2, the fourth exposure point), video-pretrained SkyReels-V2 DF 1.3B in its own autoencoder (exploratory, 10k updates of batch 8, running on GPU 3, due Sep 17 morning). Compute-matched 2.5k-update runs of the image rows (80k samples) queue behind PixArt's evaluation on GPU 2.

**Audits in flight (Sep 16):** motion-magnitude test of the rollouts (stable vs static); 15-map unseen corpus (Freedoom maps 18 to 32, 10 seeded episodes each) for zero-shot transfer scoring of every finished row; compute-matched short runs. Done: blur control, late-drop audit, episode-level bootstrap CIs, trajectory identity check.

**Timeline:** PixArt numbers Sep 16 night; video row and 17-map scores Sep 17; UniDiffuser trained by Sep 19 and evaluated Sep 20; draft Sep 20 (skeleton being written under paper/); final Sep 28; deadline Sep 30 AoE. Release: dataset shards and weights under Rohan's Hugging Face account before the final.

**Open for Rohan:** Hugging Face account for the release; whether to commit Superman cards; human study (deferred; likely skipped).

PlayGen already uses a DiT for Doom and action-aware evaluation, so retire older first-Doom-DiT language. Details and source checks: `.claude/analyses/astra-contribution-2026-09-16.md`. **Sep 10 launch-gate follow-up:** review of 1106c97 accepts the accumulation skip/continue logic for the adopted positive clipping threshold. Remaining narrow fixes: compute a real norm when clipping is disabled; preserve skip count across recovery. New-corpus reproducibility requires seeding the newly constructed ViZDoom instance before init and resetting episode-local anti-stuck state, as well as Python/NumPy/Torch RNGs. Recommendations below; no training/recorder code edited in this review.

**Sep 10 follow-up:** Rohan's requested headline evaluation is PSNR, LPIPS, FVD16/32 and a small human study; IDM is exploratory. Round-4 pairing recommends 24 raters x 60 balanced real-versus-generated trials (14/28 decision frames), fresh test-only episodes because the old held-out set informed development, and one additional full DiT seed if only one A6000-day is available. These protocol recommendations await comparison and agreement.

**Sep 10 round-3 pairing review (recommendations, not adopted):** owner decisions remain corrected-transition warm-start restarts, decision-rate latents, and the existing frozen encoder / fine-tuned decoder. Independent review recommends shared lr 5e-5, warmup 2000, batch 32, L32; recovery and validation fixes before launch; K8 IDM with a matched-sequence real-data reference; A6000 placement when free. Findings and unresolved choices are logged below. No training code was changed by this review.

**Project goal (finalized Sep 8, 2026).** DoomDiT rebuilds a GameNGen-style Doom world model and asks one question: what changes when the U-Net backbone is replaced by a Diffusion Transformer. DiTs have become the default in industry video and world models, but no controlled comparison exists for action-conditioned game simulation. We train both backbones on the same data, latents, and conditioning under the same small compute budget (16 GB GPUs, tens of GPU-hours), so the backbone is the only variable between our two rows. We do not attempt GameNGen's scale; GameNGen enters as a reference point, not a matched baseline. We measure per-frame quality, autoregressive stability, action following, and training feasibility, release the lossless 320x240 Doom dataset we build, and target the CoRL 2026 PhysWM workshop on Sep 30. Multi-map generalization and memory (MultiGen) are the follow-up paper.

**Target: CoRL 2026 PhysWM workshop, Sep 30 AoE, 4 pages** (working assumption from Rohan's Sep 1 lean; confirm at the Sep 2 1-on-1). The Sep 2 and Sep 5 NeurIPS workshops are out: the Superman project directory is gone and nothing can be re-measured in four days. ICLR 2027 (Sep 24) needs the full experiment set and is not reachable either. Section 0c is the experiment list written backwards from Sep 30.

**The paper** is the controlled U-Net vs DiT world-model comparison under matched data and compute, plus the small-compute recipe (section 3). Two facts found on Sep 1 change what "the final run" means:

1. The released checkpoint trained on all 500 episodes, and the 26.04 dB / 0.153 headline was scored on the training-time eval segments, which are training data. There is no honest held-out number for it. **Both backbones must be retrained on a 450-episode split**; the final DiT run is not optional.
2. Superman holds nothing from April except the `Doom` conda env and logs. Latents, runs 004/012, and any U-Net artifacts were deleted (disk cleanup; home is now LEGO and perseve work). Weights are safe on the GitHub release. The source dataset is 48 GB of JPEG parquet on Hugging Face; `encode_episodes.py` (new) rebuilds per-episode latents from it in a few GPU-hours.

**Sep 9 state (morning, after the overnight run):** the data-collection agent is Arnold (paper-backed, MultiGen's choice) recording every tic losslessly on 17 maps; 850 episodes recorded, encoded, split, and on both servers; the 1,000-episode Stiegler set is the continuity set, and the April checkpoint reproduces on it (27.4 dB / 0.117 held-out, copy-last 26.2 dB). The new stack (velocity objective, L = 32 stacked context, GameNGen noise augmentation, one recipe for both backbones) is validated on real data and **both main runs are training on Superman**: `010-dit-l32` (four GPUs, 0.9 steps/s, done about Sep 10 06:00) and `011-unet-l32` (one GPU, resumed from 5k after an OOM at checkpoint save, done about Sep 13). The DiT context sweep, the IDM (ceiling 69.3% top-1), and decoder fine-tuning (23.7 to 29.1 dB, HUD 17.9 to 34.7; an LPIPS variant is training) are done or in flight. Early DiT numbers at step 15k with the fine-tuned decoder: 20.3 dB / 0.356 LPIPS held-out, above the 19.7 dB copy-last baseline; the U-Net's held-out v-loss at 5k (0.2445) is below the DiT's (0.2552), so the comparison is live. Evaluation scripts, figures, paper skeleton, data cards, and the Hugging Face release script exist. What remains: let the runs finish, run the M3 checklist (`REQUIREMENTS.md` section 10), fill the tables, write. Open for Rohan: HF account for the upload, a second seed, and the Friday update (draft in `docs/updates/`).

**Why it matters beyond the paper:** the same action-conditioned latent DiT is the starting point for fall goal 3, a world model for LEGO trajectory decisions, and Changliu's May 4 suggestion of action rollouts for long-horizon planning plus sim-switching at contact-rich points using the perseve Isaac Sim pipeline. `doom_data.py` and `doomdit_utils.py` are written to be reused there.

**Standing rhythm:** weekly 1-on-1 with Changliu every Wednesday 1:00 to 1:30 PM from Sep 2 to Dec 9; Friday Slack update tagged by goal.

## 0b. How we got here (condensed)

DoomDiT is an action-conditioned latent diffusion world model: DiT-XL/2 (673M params) predicts the next VizDoom frame from 4 past frame latents plus one discrete action, trained on Superman (4× A4000 16 GB, about 72 GPU-hours) as the CMU 18-789 final project with Keerthana Chirumamilla. Best checkpoint is step 87,200, loss 0.0481, released as GitHub tag `002-DiT-XL-2-best-90k`. Headline teacher-forced numbers: DiT 26.04 dB PSNR / 0.153 LPIPS vs a matched U-Net baseline 24.60 / 0.198 (GameNGen reports 29.43 / 0.249, but that is a cross-paper comparison). The defensible contribution is the **controlled U-Net vs DiT comparison under matched data and compute plus the small-compute recipe**, not "first DiT world model" (Oasis, Matrix-Game, NFD, SANA-WM already exist). Nothing has been trained or evaluated since April 22. The fall goal is a workshop paper by mid October.

Compute anchor: DiT-XL/2 ran at 1.62 steps/s at global batch 32 on 4 A4000s, so 90k steps is about 15.5 h; the U-Net is assumed comparable. Six GPUs are usable at most (GPUs 0 and 3 are occupied by others on Sep 1), so DiT on four and U-Net on two, or the two runs back to back.

| Sep 12 to 19 | **Final runs launched by Sep 15 (DiT) and Sep 16 (U-Net)**: 450-episode split, identical recipe, at least 90k steps each. Warm-start ablation: DiT-XL/2 from scratch for 20k steps vs warm-started for 20k steps (about 3.5 h each). | 4 + 2 GPUs, 3 days |

Fallback if the final runs slip past Sep 19: report the released checkpoint on the legacy segments with the training-data caveat stated in the text, keep the U-Net row from April as "reported in the class project, not reproduced", and lead with the autoregressive metrics on the DiT. That is a weaker paper; the point of launching by Sep 15 is not to need it.

Disk budget on Superman (98 GB free on Sep 1, other users hold the rest): latents 5.9 GB, raw frames for ten held-out episodes about 2.8 GB, released weights 5.2 GB, parquet shards streamed one at a time (0.5 GB), two final runs with `--keep-last 3` plus `best.pt` about 21 GB, IDM and eval outputs under 2 GB. **About 37 GB total**, leaving roughly 60 GB free. The 250 GB rule cannot be met by us because the shortfall is other people's data; this needs Rohan's explicit go-ahead.

## 2. What is missing for a paper (in priority order)

1. **U-Net baseline code and checkpoints**: not in the repo, not in any transcript. Recover from Superman or Keerthana, or re-train a matched-params U-Net from the documented recipe.
2. **PSNR/LPIPS harness**: rebuilt Sep 1 as `eval_metrics.py`; April numbers reproduced Sep 9 (27.43 dB / 0.117 on lossless held-out frames of the same agent, copy-last 26.24 / 0.141). New-stack harness is `eval_tf.py`.
3. **Autoregressive metrics**: none exist. Design in `.claude/analyses/autoregressive-eval-design.md` (drift curves, FVD16/32, IDM accuracy; one rollout engine feeding all three). Teacher-forced-only evaluation is the most likely rejection reason.
4. **No validation split, single seed**; `best.pt` was picked by smoothed train loss and trained on every episode. Split policy now lives in `doom_data.make_split` (episode-level, seeded, 10% held out); the released checkpoint cannot be scored on it honestly.
5. README is still upstream fast-DiT; presentation claims gradient checkpointing was on but the code default is off; every comment says "DDIM" but sampling is respaced ancestral DDPM (`p_sample_loop`).
6. Inference is 50-step DDIM at about 5 s per 8 frames. Real time is out of scope for the workshop paper; state it as future work (Self-Forcing / CausVid style distillation).

## 3. Paper plan (decided July 15, reaffirmed Aug 21)

- **Spine 1**: controlled backbone ablation, U-Net vs DiT, same VAE, same conditioning, same data, same compute.
- **Spine 2**: small-compute reproducibility, about 72 GPU-hours on 16 GB cards, full recipe and weights released.
- **Supporting ablation**: ImageNet image-model warm start vs from scratch (cheap; warm start gave a 20× loss drop in the first 1,000 steps).
- **Future work**: cross-game transfer with weight-level analysis (which layers move: adaLN action pathway vs spatial blocks), differentiated from GameFactory; scenario-latent skill files (`IDEAS.md`); robotics grasp-candidate rejection (recap section 8).
- Must-cite and differentiate: GameNGen, DIAMOND, Diffusion Forcing, Oasis, Next-Frame Diffusion (310M AR DiT, closest cousin), MineWorld (IDM metric), Matrix-Game (GameWorld Score), GameFactory. arXiv IDs in the agent context brief.

## 4. Fall 2026 goal and timeline (submitted Aug 21)

Goal 1 of 4: *DoomDiT world model to a final result, workshop paper by mid October. Evaluate the current checkpoint vs the U-Net / GameNGen baseline, plan and run the final larger model and dataset run, write up as a controlled U-Net vs DiT comparison at small compute. Stretch: one world model learning multiple scenarios or games with a game flag.*

|---|---|

| Sep 14 to Oct 2 | Launch the final run, start the workshop paper. |
| Oct 5 to 16 | Workshop paper submitted. |

Rohan's Sep 1 framing: get the workshop paper together in the next three to four weeks. Risk stated in the plan: if the final run needs more compute or time than Superman allows, fall back to the current checkpoint plus the new metrics.

No single run isolates architecture/pretraining/context simultaneously. Verify original-model parity after inflation, both conditioning-token gradients/dependence, real velocity-loss optimizer steps, checkpoint/EMA reload and evaluation routing, and measured completion time including evaluation. Live/EMA reporting and compute accounting still need finalization. Any DiT-only LR branch requires a tested post-resume LR override and separate tuning accounting.

Current committed anti-stuck preamble is deterministic and upstream DQN next_action is greedy; do not introduce exploration when adding seeds. Adoption of exact recorder integration awaits owner implementation and real-server replay checks.

- **Sep 10 follow-up, pending agreement:** human-study assignment and episode/rater-clustered analysis; fresh test-only collection versus transparently quarantined historical holdouts; one full DiT seed as the single-run robustness check. Accept 5000-step recovery cadence given measured 2–3 minute upload cost; reshuffled data order is acceptable for crash recovery if disclosed, but DDP must not restore rank 0's RNG on every rank. No claim of exact trajectory replay without sampler position and independent rank RNG.

- **Sep 10 round-3 review, pending comparison with Rohan:** shared lr 5e-5 / warmup 2000; live versus EMA selection under one fixed policy; freeze the 850-episode main pair and prioritize a paired second seed over a data-scale training ablation; obtain free A6000 slots versus protected Superman fallback. Distinguish equal updates/examples from equal measured compute. Before relaunch, resolve incomplete recovery, batch-dependent validation corruption, and disk-failure handling; freeze untouched episode-level test data before further selection.

- **Sep 9 independent pairing recommendation (not adopted):** frame the workshop as a budget-constrained comparison of two pretrained backbone packages, with outcome-independent acceptance criteria. Prioritize paired final evaluation, action interventions, episode-level uncertainty, and measured compute accounting; demote extra training ablations and FVD if its implementation cannot be validated. Existing winner-required requirements R4.5/R6.1 need discussion, not silent enforcement.

1. **Deadline**: Sep 30 CoRL PhysWM (assumed) vs ICLR 2027 Sep 24 vs slipping to a later workshop. Decides everything in section 0c.
2. **Disk go-ahead**: about 37 GB on a Superman disk that is already at 98 GB free. The 250 GB rule is unmeetable by us; proceed or find another machine (Spiderman has 3 TB free but 4 A6000s shared with perseve).
3. **Data path**: re-encode from Hugging Face with `encode_episodes.py` (48 GB streamed, 3 to 5 GPU-hours, exact match to Keerthana's latents not guaranteed) vs waiting for the Drive zip. Recommendation: start the re-encode now, swap in the zip if it arrives, because the final runs need latents either way and the re-encode makes the repo self-contained.
4. **Matched U-Net design**: match parameter count (about 670M, a 2D U-Net with the same 20-in / 8-out channel contract and adaLN-style action+time conditioning) or match training FLOPs; warm-start from SD 1.4 or not. Recommendation: parameter-matched, both backbones from scratch for the main row, ImageNet warm start as a DiT-only supporting row. The April comparison's warm-start status is unknown until Keerthana answers.
5. **Action offset**: training used the action stored on the target frame's row (`ACTION_OFFSET = 4` in `doom_data.py`). Whether that is the causally right action depends on the dataset's logging convention. Keep it for reproduction; decide before the final runs.
6. Whether the multi-game stretch belongs in this paper at all (recommendation: future work).

- **2026-09-26 16:50 EDT: contribution framing with Keerthana: shared physics, different appearance; the unseen-map gap gets decomposed into physics, latent appearance and rendering.** From Rohan and Keerthana's discussion (transcript in the chat): the candidate contributions were the backbone comparison (not novel), a post-training fix for the collapses (no time), the map-distance predictor (Changliu's suggestion, now qualified evidence), and "cheap adaptation to an unseen map by fine-tuning only the decoder". Main's assessment: the decoder never touches the simulation (rollouts run in latent space), so decoder-only adaptation is a rendering gain and cannot be claimed as learning a new map; turned around, the idea becomes a decomposition of the unseen-map gap: (1) physics, tested by the directional check on every evaluation map (does the model still turn the right way by the right amount in rooms it never saw); (2) latent appearance, the backbone's latent error against copy-last in latent space (the new decoder-free ratio in eval_tf); (3) rendering, the same predicted latents decoded by the stock decoder, the training-map-tuned decoder and a decoder tuned on the unseen map's own footage. Hypothesis (Rohan): the engine and controls are identical on every map, so the physics should transfer and the loss should sit in appearance; the distance metric predicts the appearance loss. Finalised contribution sentence: world models trained on a few maps are evaluated almost only in-domain; we build the first multi-map Doom benchmark with a persistence reference, show three backbones under one recipe fall to copy-last-frame on unseen maps, show a training-to-map distance predicts the degradation, and decompose the gap into physics, latent appearance and rendering; supporting: the matched comparison as the systems layer, the closed-loop stability rates, the directional check. - **2026-09-24 21:55 EDT: the native W&B init hang is root-caused, fixed and verified live (`11540db` on main).** Cause (worker, reproduced with the real SDK 0.30.0 offline): inside `wandb.init` the SDK starts subprocesses on the calling thread (`git rev-parse` in `wandb/sdk/lib/gitlib.py:57` via `wandb_init.py:324` and `:933`, `uname -p` from `platform.platform()` in `wandb_settings.py:1628`, the `wandb-core` service in `service_process.py:149`) and waits for end-of-file on each pipe; the trainer's main thread forks its persistent DataLoader workers at the same moment, a worker forked inside that window inherits the pipe's write end and never closes it (it never execs), so the read never sees end-of-file and init hangs for the life of the run. The live signature (core log "accepting connections", "new connection created", then nothing; no run directory; no "View run" line) is reproduced exactly by forcing a fork during `uname -p`. Fix: `RunLogger` calls `wandb.setup()` and `platform.platform(aliased=True)` on the caller's thread in its constructor, before the trainer forks (0.24 s, no network), opens runs with `disable_git=True`, and installs a backstop audit hook that refuses any subprocess started on the logger thread; a real-SDK subprocess test forks eight workers during init and passes in 0.8 s where the old code hung 60 s. Suite 1,424 (1 skip: the real-SDK test where wandb is absent). Verified on Spiderman in the doom env with the real SDK online: a RunLogger under eight forking DataLoader workers created its run, logged 16 rows, closed in 1.7 s (check run deleted). - **2026-09-19 20:00 EDT: CORRECTION, frame spacing. The Sep 10 claim that GameNGen and its reproduction predict every 4th frame is wrong.** Evidence card `lit/frame-spacing-prior-work.md` (primary sources, quotes). The open reproduction stores and trains on CONSECUTIVE tics: `load_model_generate_dataset.py:132-133` sets `frame_skip = 1` ("capture all frames"), chooses an action every 4th tic and repeats it; `skipframe-4` names the action repeat, no frames are dropped; its training window is 10 consecutive tics (0.29 s). GameNGen states action repeat 4 (App. A.5) and never states a training stride; the best-supported reading is consecutive 35 Hz tics (App. A.6: "teacher-forced trajectories on 35FPS data"), and its frames-to-seconds conversions at 20 fps are internally inconsistent, so do not cite its context length in seconds. Consequence: its 29.43 dB is most likely a next-TIC teacher-forced number and is not comparable to our next-decision (4-tic) numbers; on our footage persistence alone is 21.3 dB at 1 tic against 18.8 at 4. Field pattern: RL world models (SimPLe, DIAMOND Atari, STORM) step per decision and discard skipped frames; neural game engines (GameNGen, MineWorld, Matrix-Game 2.0) predict every rendered frame with the action repeated; video-trained models subsample for sequence length (GAIA-1 6.25 Hz, WHAM and Genie 10 Hz). No game paper ablates spacing; NWM reports 1 fps better short-horizon and 4 fps better past 8 s; Vid2World's head-to-head tables are unread. The two persistence numbers in our notes do not conflict: 26.2 dB per tic (R1.3, Sep 9) was the Stiegler PPO footage, 21.3 dB is Arnold deathmatch footage. Actions: per-tic latents of the original and evaluation corpora are being encoded overnight into new directories so a matched stride 1 / 2 / 4 experiment can launch after Rohan approves the design; R1.3's rationale stands as our choice but its literature basis is withdrawn. Open decision for Rohan: training stride. Tagged: goal 1.

- **2026-09-19 15:30 EDT: why GameNGen's PSNR is high, and the dense 4-map corpus (recording).** Measured on our raw seen evaluation frames, persistence PSNR by frame gap: 1 tic 21.3 dB, 2 tics 20.1, 4 tics (our decision spacing) 18.8, 8 tics 18.1; so the footage is violently dynamic even one tic apart. - **2026-09-16 15:10 EDT: red-team review (`.claude/analyses/red-team-2026-09-16.md`) and two fixes.** Its strongest points: (1) "exposure predicts quality" is not monotone in the paper's own numbers (PixArt at 25M images beats the U-Net at about 2B on held-out loss; under presentations rather than unique images the ImageNet DiT saw about 1.8B), so the claim must be about the pretraining source (class-conditional ImageNet vs text-conditioned web-scale) rather than a quantity law; (2) the abstract's "weakest start on every metric" is false (the DiT wins rollout PSNR@64, unseen PSNR, degradation); (3) the compute-matched 2.5k-update runs used warmup 2000 and would have measured warmup, fixed now to warmup 250 and relaunched; (4) the VAE decoder was fine-tuned on seen-map training frames and its ceiling drops 2.25 dB on unseen maps (28.61 to 26.36), so transfer must be reported next to the ceiling's own degradation; (5) Freedoom MAP18 to 32 are single-player levels, a different design family from the 17 deathmatch arenas, which the transfer section must state; (6) the persistence finding wants an inference-time context-noise sweep (bucket 0 was never tuned; 0.6 card-days) before it is called a mechanism. Recommended spend of the remaining card-days: LR forks, context-noise sweep, UniDiffuser to 90k, unseen-map rollouts, a second U-Net seed. Its rewritten abstract is in the file. Framing adjustment adopted: headline becomes "the pretraining source, not the architecture, decides small-budget Doom world-model quality; the standard class-conditional ImageNet DiT is the weakest start on perceptual quality, and the same skeleton with text-image pretraining matches the SD U-Net", with the transfer and persistence findings carrying their caveats.

## 8. Technical facts most likely to matter (full detail in the agent context brief)

- Objective check against the papers (Sep 14): GameNGen trains v-prediction with an L2 loss on a linear schedule, DDIM sampling, actions as one cross-attention token, MSE decoder fine-tune; MultiGen trains the standard velocity objective with actions through cross-attention and context frames on channels; the original DiT trains eps-prediction plus a variational term on a learned variance, which we disable. Our loss therefore matches both Doom papers exactly and differs from DiT's own paper by design.

**As built, Sep 14 (read from the code; supersedes the older bullets below where they disagree).** Third backbone (Sep 14): PixArt-alpha 512 transformer, 611.6M, same 132-channel input, action + noise bucket as two cross-attention tokens through the pretrained caption projection, timestep via adaLN-single, epsilon half of the 8-channel head retrained as velocity; recipe identical to the pair. Freedoom assets on ViZDoom, Arnold's deathmatch maps 1 to 17, 8 bots, crosshair on, 29 actions held 4 tics. Corpus 850 episodes; split 675 / 75 / 100 (maps 16, 17); fresh seeded reporting corpus 60 + 20 episodes. Training frames are verified transitions (1,054,647 in 7,296 chains). Latents `sd-vae-ft-mse`, 320x256 padding, 4x32x40, fp16; decoder fine-tuned MSE + 0.1 LPIPS. Input: 32 context latents (128 ch) + noisy target (4) = 132 channels, action of the transition into the target, noise bucket. DiT-XL/2 (675M, adaLN-Zero on t + action + bucket, learned variance off, 4-ch output, ImageNet-256 warm start); SD 1.4 U-Net (860M, action as one cross-attention token, bucket via class embedding). Objective: velocity prediction, MSE on v, linear betas 1e-4 to 0.02 over 1,000 steps, uniform t. Context noise augmentation U(0, 0.7) with 10 buckets; inference context clean. Action dropout 0, no CFG. AdamW lr 5e-5, warmup 2,000, wd 0, clip 1.0, global batch 32, bf16 autocast with fp32 master, fp32 EMA 0.9999 (diagnostic), 90k updates, seed 0. Validation every 1k on 1,024 per-window-seeded held-out windows; recovery checkpoints every 5k on Spiderman. Sampling DDIM 50, eta 0. Metrics: teacher-forced PSNR / LPIPS / HUD PSNR vs raw frames on 2,048 windows (seen and unseen corpora), copy-last and VAE ceiling; 256 x 64 rollouts with drift, copy-seed, FVD 16 / 32; VPT-style IDM (K=8, 82.9% real reference) with matched real-data reference per horizon and a K=2 check. Same table lives in the plan artifact, section T.

- Dataset scale for the paper's data section (checked against the papers Sep 10): GameNGen trains on a random subset of 70M examples from 50M environment steps of agent play at 320x240 padded to 320x256, context 64. MultiGen (arXiv 2603.06679) collects over 10M frames with the Lample and Chaplot agent on 100 Obsidian-generated maps for level design, and over 10M frames of 1-vs-4 deathmatch on a single map for multiplayer; it does not state resolution, frame skip, train/test map split, base model, steps, batch, or sampler. Its context ablation: L=2/4/8/16/32 gives PSNR 27.6/29.5/29.8/29.8/30.0 and LPIPS 0.121/0.097/0.094/0.093/0.089, diminishing above 8. Ours: 1.05M decision frames (4.2M tics) on 17 maps, so 10x smaller than MultiGen and 70x smaller than GameNGen in frames; the recorder is restartable if more is wanted.
- Training windows in the current runs (010, 011, 020) sit on the tic%4 grid; 39% of them straddle a decision boundary. The corrected encoding (`--align-decisions`) keeps 99.4% of frames and gives every window a single verified 4-tic action; nothing has been re-encoded or relaunched yet.
- Latents from `stabilityai/sd-vae-ft-mse`, frames 160×120 → (4, 15, 20), height padded to 16, 80 tokens at patch 2.
- Context by channel concatenation: 4 past latents (16 ch) + noisy target (4 ch) → `in_channels=20`; `learn_sigma=True`.
- Action via repurposed `LabelEmbedder` (18 VizDoom actions), `c = t_emb + action_emb` → adaLN-Zero; 10% dropout retained; CFG available but unused.
- Data: HF `arnaudstiegler/vizdoom-500-episodes-skipframe-4-lvl5`: 97 parquet shards, 48 GB, 2,470,229 rows of (episode_id, JPEG frame, action, health, step_id), rows grouped by episode. 500 episodes minus 4 windows each gives the N = 2,468,229 of the old consolidated arrays. `doom_data.EpisodeWindowDataset` indexes per-episode files directly; `build_dataset.py` is kept only for the legacy layout.
- Recipe: global batch 32, fused AdamW lr 1e-4, wd 0, warmup 500, grad clip 1.0, bf16, DDPM 1000 linear; in-training and eval sampling is respaced ancestral DDPM at 50 steps via `p_sample_loop` (the code and docs call it DDIM; `--sampler ddim` in `eval_metrics.py` is the true DDIM); EMA 0.9999 in fp32 math.
- Companion projects: `~/Documents/Github/perseve` (adversarial SDG) and `~/Documents/Github/Lego_Manipulation` (LEGO seg + pose). The fall plan's goal 3 carries this world model into LEGO trajectory decisions using the Isaac Sim pipeline from perseve.
